# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2020-07-01_TotalExtraction_10573498.pdf
  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
July 13, 2020 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending electronic conference meeting were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.    
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Mr. White moved to approve, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 22, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Special Meeting of the Governing Body on June 29,2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for Lots 1 and 2 and a Portion of a 20 
Foot Vacated Alley being adjacent to and west of said Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Prairie Hills Addition, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of Yellowstone Road and 
Western Hills Blvd).  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been 
met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020, Members-Terms-
Vacancies, of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Amber Ash, Downtown 
Development Authority Executive Director, advised she was available for any questions.  Upon 
inquiry, Ms. Ash and Mayor Orr provided information on the application process for 
boards/commissions explaining there are often more applications received than positions 
available.  Applications are kept until there is a vacancy and the applicant is contacted to see if 
they are still interested.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case moved to approve on 
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.   Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.  (#4316) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the official zoning map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LR (Low-Density Residential) to MUR (Mixed-

  
Use Residential Emphasis) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Kelley 
Drive and Old Faithful Road.  Kari Happold, Rustic Wyoming Reality, advised she was available 
for any questions and explained Rustic Wyoming Reality is looking to expand.  Mr. Luna 
welcomed Ms. Happold to Ward III.  Tom Jacobson, #1 Properties, owner representative, voiced 
support for the ordinance.  Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4317) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Amending the official zoning map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LI (Light Industrial) to MUB (Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of west 21st Street 
and Reed Avenue. Matt Ashby, agent representing owner, advised he was available for any 
questions.   Mr. Case moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4318) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the official zoning map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis for a portion of the SE1/4 of Section 33, T.14N., R66W, 6th P.M., Cheyenne, 
Wyoming (located south of and adjacent to Sparks Road, west of Ridge Road). Jim Grenfell, 
Executive Director of Wyoming Housing Network, provided information on the land and 
explained the anticipated use includes senior living units with garages and adequate parking.  Mr. 
Grenfell advised he has had discussions with Environmental Health regarding the potential to place 
a clinic on the property.  He further advised it is anticipated that the remaining 6 acres will be used 
for future multi-family low income housing units.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 5.04, Business Licenses, Generally, 
and Chapter 5.08, Business License fees, and creating a new Chapter 5.77, Nicotine Product Sales, 
of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations; amending Chapter 8.64, Smoking in Public Places, 
of Title 8, Health and Safety; and amending Chapter 9.16, Offenses by or Against Minors, of Title 
9, Public Peace and Welfare, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Brittany 
Wardle expressed appreciation to the governing body for consideration of the ordinance and 
advised that the proposed licensing allows restrictive measures that can be utilized to help 
eliminate underage nicotine sales.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve 
on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. White advised the ordinance reflects State Statute 
modifications raising the age from 18 to 21 for purchasing tobacco/nicotine products.  Mr. White 
advised the Wyoming State Legislature did the same and noted that he, Ms. Wardle, Joe Chenchar, 
Assistant City Attorney, and a representative of the American Cancer Society reviewed the 
proposed ordinance changes.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White advised it is his understanding there were 
no comments received from small business owners or the Police Department regarding the 
proposed changes.  Joe Chenchar, Assistant City Attorney, upon inquiry, advised he will seek 
verification of businesses being notified regarding the proposed changes.  Request for clarification 
of the proposed changes vs. existing law was made and noted it appears there is a new license 
requirement and an increased fee, banning of vending machines and new eligibility requirements 
for licensees.  Mr. Chenchar advised the existing annual tobacco license fee is $65.00, noting the 
proposed increased fee was determined to assist with compliance check expenses.  Upon inquiry, 

  
Mr. Chenchar provided information relating to the appeal process involving annual violations, 
noting it is the decision of the governing body to pursue suspension/revocation of a license.  Mr. 
Chenchar confirmed convictions would be through the Municipal Court.  Support was expressed 
for the ordinance and comment was made related to timing of raising license fees considering the 
current COVID-19 hardships on small businesses.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Esquibel, 
Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mayor 
Orr, Mr. Roybal, and Mr. Shanor. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and 
Parking Generally, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming. Mr. Case moved to withdraw the ordinance.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of 
order that the item could not be withdrawn stating that after an agenda item has been introduced, 
it belongs to the governing body and can’t be withdrawn.  Mayor Orr asked if there were any 
objections to the motion to withdraw, to which Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne expressed objection.    
Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, reported that after a quick review Roberts Rules of Order and 
concurrence from Alessandra McCoy-Fakelman, Deputy City Attorney, it would be appropriate to 
vote the motion to withdraw down.  Motion to withdraw failed.    Voting “no” – all members of 
the governing body.  [Clerk’s note: the item died due to the lack of a motion to approve]      
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northwest of the intersection of Western Hills Boulevard and Yellowstone Road – referred 
to Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. CASE) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County MU (Mixed-Use) to MUB (Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis) for property generally located northwest of the intersection of Western Hills 
Boulevard and Yellowstone Road – referred to Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. 
CASE) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 2.29, Community Service Officers, 
of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming - – referred to Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. CASE) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
non-uniformed employees of the City for Cheyenne for Fiscal Year 2020-2021 – referred to 
Finance Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. WHITE) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Defining the terms and conditions of employment for 
sworn officers of the Cheyenne Police Department for Fiscal Year 2020-2021 – referred to Finance 
Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. WHITE) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Creating a Special District for ‘Cheyenne Days – Legendary Nights 
Events’, July 17, 2020 to July 26, 2020, in which the possession of open containers is permitted 
within the Downtown District Boundaries.  Amber Ash, Executive Director of the Downtown 
Development Authority, explained there were initially several events planned but due to the 
COVID-19 pandemic, sponsors of the events made the decision to cancel them.  Ms. Ash reported 

  
the DDA evaluated the costs associated with the open container event and it was determined those 
costs weren’t justified without major events taking place.  Ms. Ash further advised the Paint 
Slingers asked to remove their event from the event list along with concerts that were planned on 
that Wednesday and Friday.  Ms. Ash requested the item be withdrawn if possible or otherwise 
disposed of noting however, that a negative vote is not desired as future events could be considered.  
Comments included: concurrence of the event no longer being feasible, consumption area 
including entire DDA districts, special district resolution being a “pilot program”, support of the 
resolution in hopes it may help businesses in the area, management and enforcement for these 
types of activities and suggestions for more defined boundaries.  Ms. Ash advised she hasn’t 
specifically consulted with other communities about their open container events and that the DDA 
is looking to re-evaluate the district in the future to use the Main Street District to define 
consumption boundaries as it is smaller and more contained.  Mr. Luna moved to adopt, seconded 
by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. Luna moved to amend by substitute dated July 8, 2020 seconded by Mr. 
Roybal.  Mr. White explained the amendment includes removing the Paint Slingers event from the 
event listing and increasing the event cup size from 12 ounces to 16 ounces.  Chief Brian Kozak, 
Police Department, explained it was his understanding that the intent of the open container 
approval was for a specific downtown area that could be effectively monitored, and each eligible 
establishment would be provided with a certain number of event wristbands and cups.  Ms. Ash 
advised the events that are currently scheduled for Cheyenne Day are the Melodrama, Dueling 
Pianos and a potential block party and further advised the DDA will be promoting merchant sales 
and sidewalk sales.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
with exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.  Chief Kozak clarified that there would be no 
additional police officer expense for Cheyenne Day as it is covered under their annual budget and 
noted open consumption without specific planned events may be less than ideal. Additional 
comments were made supporting the intent of the resolution, concerns with issues raised regarding 
costs and enforcement, lack of participation and current public health environment.  Motion as 
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. 
Laybourn, Mayor Orr, and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.  [Clerk’s note: Dr. Rinne confirmed he was a 
“no” vote which wasn’t acknowledged due to a delayed audio response involving electronic 
meeting lag time]  (#6071)    
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Encouraging Summer Events at the Cheyenne Depot Plaza.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6072)   
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Certifying the Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan – referred to 
Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. CASE) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Hawk’s 
Point Subdivision, a plat of a portion of the SE ¼, Section 33, T.14N, R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, 
Laramie County,  Wyoming (located between Lincolnway and Nationway, west of Ridge Road)  
– referred to Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. CASE) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Stanfield Addition, 4th Filing, a replat of Lots 1-4, Block 652, Stanfield Addition, Cheyenne, 
Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southwest corner of the intersection of Thomes Avenue 

  
and W. 6th Street – referred to Public Services Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. CASE) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Stanfield Addition, 5th Filing, a replat of Lots 4-6, Block 693, Stanfield Addition and Lots 1-3, 
Block 694, Stanfield Addition, Second Filing, and all of those remaining portions of Lots 4 and 5, 
Stanfield Addition, Second Filing, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located at the southeast 
corner of the intersection of Stanfield Avenue and W. 5th Street) – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. CASE) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Declaring as surplus a City-owned property generally located at the 
northeast corner of Morrie Avenue and East Lincolnway, together with improvements thereon, 
and authorizing the City to proceed with the process to dispose of the property pursuant to Wyo. 
Stat. §15-1-112(A) – referred to Finance Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. LUNA) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Approving the budget of the Cheyenne Downtown Development 
Authority for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2020 and ending June 30, 2021 – referred to 
Finance Committee. (SPONSOR – MR. LUNA) 
 
[CA] 
Preliminary Plat for Whitney Ranch, 3rd Filing, a plat of a portion of the SE¼, Section 33, 
T14N, R66W, 6th PM, Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of Dell Range Blvd., 
along Van Buren Avenue) – referred to Public Services Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Grant Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives Foster

Contract between the Wyoming Department of Family Services and the City of Cheyenne,

 [CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7155 Sponsorship Agreement between the City of 
Cheyenne and Spradley Barr Toyota. (revenue to the City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(revenue to City in the amount of $35,000.00). (#7155) 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #3 to Contract #6885 between the City of Cheyenne and Simon

provide additional funds for unforeseen preparatory work to the underlying road surface  (2015-
2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed 
$65,000.00). (#6885) 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-26-20 for furnishing one (1) new 2020-2021 Motorized 
Articulated Trash Compactor with straight blade and heavy duty trash guarding for the City of 
Cheyenne Landfill (Solid Waste Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from 
Wyoming Machinery Co., Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $483,461.64). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-17-20 for furnishing one Panasonic PT-RZ21K Projector with 
lens and accessories for the City of Cheyenne Civic Center.  (Recreation Program Fund).  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Brown Note Productions, Thornton, CO, in an 
amount not to exceed $51,142.86). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-14-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Simon 
Contractors for the Prairie Avenue & Frontier Mall Drive Intersection Project.  (2015-2018 1% 
Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from Simon Contractors, 
Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $1,339,846.50). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-13-20 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and JTL Group 
Inc. d/b/a Knife River for the Allison Road & Cribbon Avenue Median Improvements Project 
(2015-2018 1% Sales Tax and WYDOT Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Request to expand retail liquor license package store dimensions for Avanti, Inc. d/b/a Dell 
Range Liquor, 3806 Dell Range Blvd. – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
Appointments of Anthony Ortiz, Katherine Brady and Sara Weber to the Downtown 
Development Authority Board and Jason Powell to the Contractor Licensing Board.  Mr. White 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote (mayoral appointments).     
  
 
Under other business: 
 
 
Margaret Thompson submitted a petition with signatures of residents living within in her 
building opposing the Saturday Cruise Nights relating to excessive noise during the events. Ms. 
Thompson shared her experiences with excessive noise and advised she has a noise meter that she 
has used to record excessively high readings.  She further commented on and provided information 

  
on noise limits as sited in current city code and requested consideration for an alternative location 
for the event.  Mayor Orr expressed appreciation to Ms. Thompson for bringing forward a 
recommendation for an alternate location.  Chief Kozak advised the Police Department does have 
a sound meter and reported enforcement in this case is difficult due to the nature of this particular 
event as there are multiple vehicles involved and there isn’t a particular event organizer or entity 
sponsoring the event.  Chief Kozak further reported violations of this particular city code section 
are difficult to enforce.  Mr. Laybourn expressed concerns relating to Ms. Thompson’s and 
accompanying petitioner’s complaint and supported efforts to reach a resolution.     
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:16 p.m. 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
July 22, 2020 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2020-02-04_TotalExtraction_55568924.pdf
  
 
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 10, 2020 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky 
Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent: Rocky Case and Bryan Cook.  Also present: Kris Jones, City 
Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Luna and Mr. Roybal, agenda item #16 (c) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. 
Luna moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on January 27, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for a portion of the S½ of Section 35, 
and a portion of the SW¼ of Section 36, T14N, R67W Laramie County, Wyoming (located 
between Old Happy Jack Road and Happy Jack Road, west of I-25). Seth Lloyd, Planning and 
Development Department, advised the City has initiated the annexation, provided information 
relating to the property and reviewed requirements of and compliance with annexation conditions 
contained in state statutes.  Mr. Lloyd reported the affiliated annexation ordinance would be 
introduced at a later date.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing having 
been met, Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a restaurant liquor license application filed with the 
City Clerk’s Office for C & Z LLC, d/b/a China Buffet, 1958 Dell Range Blvd., Suite D, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Upon inquiry, Kevin Christianson, representing C & Z LLC, explained the request is 
for a new license due to new ownership.  Mr. Christianson advised the current license will expire 
on March 31, 2020 and the new one will take effect the following day, April 1, 2020 due to the 
timing of annual license renewals and the new ownership.  Kris Jones, City Clerk, confirmed upon 
inquiry, employees are required to receive TIPS training in order to serve alcohol.  There being no 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Orr referred the item to the 
Finance Committee and closed the hearing. 
 

  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendments, 
Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map of the Unified 
Development Code, changing the zoning classification from LR Low Density Residential to NR-
2 Neighborhood Residential - Medium Density for the west half of Lot 2 and all of Lot 3, 
Sunnyside Addition, 8th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast corner of Dell Range 
Blvd. and Van Buren Ave.). Sharman Straub, area land owner, reported she did not receive 
notification of the proposed zone change, noted objection and stated the following concerns:  
multi-family development relating to small lot size, lack of current adequate drainage, roadways 
icing over during winter months, questioned whether setbacks would be vacated, parking issues 
and limitations and space restrictions involving landscaping requirements.  Ms. Straub voiced 
concerns relating to traffic access and egress relating to proposed roadway improvements on Van 
Buren Ave. Daniel Holbrook, property owner and applicant, reported he has met with some 
adjacent property owners related to concerns and advised he will meet all city code requirements 
related to parking, drainage and setbacks.  He further noted he met with the traffic engineer on 
how the Dell Range expansion will impact this project and reported it is being taken into 
consideration.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Upon inquiry related to adjacent property owner 
notification, Mr. Holbrook advised this is the first he had heard that a neighbor in the area hadn’t 
received notification. He explained he hired an engineering firm to send out required notifications 
and the firm provided information showing notice was sent to required area property owners.  He 
added he is willing to work with neighbors regarding their concerns.  Mr. Laybourn moved to 
postpone to February 24, 2020.  Charles Bloom, Planning & Development Director, reported as 
part of the proposed rezoning process, the applicant must send notice to properties within 300 ft.  
Mr. Bloom advised the City requires confirmation that all neighbors within 300 ft. receive notice 
and they believe everyone that was required to receive notice was properly noticed.  Mr. Bloom 
explained the property in question is within 300 ft.  Ms. Straub upon inquiry advised they don’t 
reside at the property and that the current tenant is her daughter who reported she did not receive 
the notice.  Motion to postpone died due to lack of second.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with exception of Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White 
voting “no”.  (Clerk note-ordinances on third and final reading require a minimum of six (6) 
affirmative votes) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapters 8.40, Food and Food Service 
Establishments, within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and Chapters 10.12, Administration and Enforcement, 10.16, Arrest and Citation 
Procedure, 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, 10.60, Parking Limitations, 10.64, 
Loading and Unloading, 10.68, Critical Traffic and Parking Areas, within Title 10, Vehicles and 
Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne Municipal Code (Postponed from 01-27-2020).  Mr. Roybal 
advised the item died at Public Services Committee due to lack of a motion.  Upon inquiry, Mr. 
Roybal explained the committee discussed division of the proposed amendments into multiple 
documents. Item died due to the lack of motion.   
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapter 5.52, Massage Establishments, within 
Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 

  
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapters 8.40, Food and Food Service 
Establishments, within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and Chapters 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, and 10.68, Critical 
Traffic and Parking Areas, within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsors – Dr. Rinne and Mr. 
Shanor) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Chapters 10.12, Administration and 
Enforcement, 10.16, Arrest and Citation Procedure, 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking 
Generally, 10.60, Parking Limitations, 10.64, Loading and Unloading, within Title 10, Vehicles 
and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsors – Dr. Rinne and Mr. Shanor) 
 
 [CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and City Clerk to accept for ownership, 
development and maintenance, land from the Sweetgrass Land Co., LLC for the purpose of 
greenway and open space use pursuant to the recorded Sweetgrass 1st Filing and Sweetgrass 2nd 
Filing subdivisions.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6037) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution No. 5979 directing the availability of the 
production of Tchaikovsky’s The Nutcracker Ballet at the Cheyenne Civic Center.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. (#6038) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Alliance 
Addition, 4th Filing, a replat of a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, Alliance Addition, Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of Ridge Road and Jackson 
Street) – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Buffalo 
Ridge Estates, 11th Filing, a replat of Lot 15, Block 21, Buffalo Ridge Estates, Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located southeast of the intersection of Hilltop Avenue and  Bluff Place) – 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, 
Wyoming and the City of Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities for the GIS Cooperative Budget 
Consolidation.  (City General Fund and County funds).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#7142/7143) 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and the Laramie County 
Conservation District for allocation of funds for planning, design and implementation of Crow 
Creek and its tributaries rehabilitation project.  (1991-1994 1% Sales Tax per Resolution #4093 – 
funds moved in 2013 to the Specific Purpose Sales Tax Fund and proceeds from purchase and sale 
agreement of Lot 1, Block 1, Pat Griffin Second Filing – funds located in the Specific Purpose 
Sales Tax Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $147,340.00). 
(#7144) 
 

  
 
Purchase and Sale Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and the Laramie County 
School District Number One, for real property described as Lots 1 through 12, Block 2, Roberts 
Place Addition, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, and more commonly known as 
610 West 7th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming, in the amount of $569,780.00, to be used as a future 
elementary school  (Revenue to the City).  Mr. Luna moved to approve in an amount not to exceed 
$569,780.00, seconded by Mr. White.  Teresa Moore, Community Recreation & Events Director, 
upon inquiry advised options are being explored relating to possible future relocation of the 
gymnastics program and noted they have had discussions with Laramie School District #1 
regarding leasing the current location until it is needed for development of a new elementary 
school.  Robin Lockman, City Treasurer, upon inquiry explained the amount of unassigned and 
unrestricted reserves is difficult to project and advised the amount changes month to month 
throughout the year.  Ms. Lockman provided additional accounting information involving revenue 
projections.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#7145) 
 
[CA] 
Development agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Brandon Swain regarding fire 
access (Northwest of Marshall Road and Storey Boulevard).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) 
carried. (#7146) 
 
[CA] 
Letter of Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, and the United 
States Department of Justice, Drug Enforcement Administration, for locating and eradicating illicit 
cannabis.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed $75,000.00 – revenue 
to City). (#7147) 
 
[CA] 
Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Blue Federal Credit Union, 
Inc., for the redevelopment and access at the Pershing Boulevard/Converse Avenue/19th Street 
Roundabout – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Audiomatrix Inc., for 
the City of Cheyenne Public, Educational, and Governmental (PEG) Access Channel 
Programming Equipment and Production Equipment Upgrade Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax, 
Charter Equipment Funding Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-7-20 for furnishing As Needed Emergency Snow Control for the 
City of Cheyenne, Public Works Department (2015-2018 & 2019-2022 1% Sales Tax).  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from JTL Group, Inc. d/b/a Knife River, Cheyenne, 
in an amount not to exceed $200,000.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-10-20 for furnishing new computers and operating systems for 
the City of Cheyenne Information Technology Division (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax).  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried (accepted bid from DHE Computer Systems, Centennial, CO, in an 
amount not to exceed $49,691.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-11-20 for furnishing one (1) new 2019-2020 ¾ ton regular cab, 
full size pickup truck and one (1) new 2019-2020 ¾ ton ext. cab, full size pickup truck for the City 
of Cheyenne, Parks Division  (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax)  -- referred to Finance Committee.  

  
 
Voucher Report.  No comments were made.   
 
 
Announcement was made that due to the President’s Day Holiday on Monday, February 
17, 2020, regularly scheduled City Council Committee meetings will instead be held in the 
Municipal Building, Room 104, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as follows: Finance 
Committee will meet Tuesday, February 18, 2020 at 11:00 a.m. and Public Services Committee 
will meet Wednesday, February, 19, 2020 at Noon. 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 24, 2020 at 6:00pm in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a microbrewery  liquor 
license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Black Tooth Brewing Company, LLC 
d/b/a/ Black Tooth Brewing, 520 W. 19th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held February 24, 2020 at 6:00pm in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for renewal applications for 
existing City liquor licenses (retail, restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, 
microbrewery, satellite distillery, winery and special malt beverage permits) filed with the City 
Clerk’s Office. 
 
 
Under other business:  
 
 
Upon inquiry, City Clerk Kris Jones clarified that ordinances on third and final reading 
require six (6) affirmative votes. 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:30 p.m. 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2020-02-04_TotalExtraction_49880744.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 11, 2020 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne convened in a special meeting on this date 
beginning at 6:00 p.m. in City Council Chambers for the purpose of meeting in Executive Session 
involving personnel matters relating to collective labor bargaining with the Cheyenne Professional 
Fire Fighters, International Association of Fire Fighters, Local No. 279.  Present were:  MAYOR 
- Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. 
Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White. Absent:  Mr. Case and Mr. Cook.  Also present: Kris Jones, 
City Clerk; Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney; Alessandra McCoy Fakelman, Deputy City 
Attorney; Robin Lockman, City Treasurer and Chief Greg Hoggatt, Fire and Rescue Department.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
  
 
  
 
Mayor Orr advised she would entertain a motion to go into executive session.  Pursuant to 
Wyoming State Statute §16-4-405(a)(x), Mr. Shanor moved to meet in executive session, seconded 
by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
Mayor Orr announced there will be no action taken by the governing body as a result of 
the executive session, therefore, the special meeting will be officially adjourned following closure 
of the executive session.  
  
Mayor Orr identified, in addition to members of the governing body present, the City Clerk, 
City Attorney, Deputy City Attorney, Fire and Rescue Department Fire Chief and the City 
Treasurer would also be present at the executive session.  [City Clerk note: Audio recording of the 
special meeting was stopped; Executive Session convened at 6:03 p.m. in Council Chambers] 
 
Following closure of the executive session, Dr. Rinne, Council President, reconvened the 
special meeting at 7:07 p.m.  [City Clerk note: Mayor Orr relinquished duties of the Chair to Dr. 
Rinne, Council President, at 6:41 p.m. due to a previous commitment] 
   
   
Dr. Rinne stated the purpose of the special meeting had been met, and there being no 
business previously noted for the special meeting, adjourned the meeting at 7:08 p.m.   
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
February 15, 2020 
  


--- END OF FILE ---
